Table 7:
The
g_{s}
scalings
\theta_{\bar{c}(r)}
and
\theta_{\hat{c}(r)}
satisfying (
184
).

We will start with the first question and to answer this we refer the readers to
Table
7
where the various distributions of
\theta_{\bar{c}(r)}
and
\theta_{\hat{c}(r)}
satisfying (
184
) are laid out. Let
{\rm M}_{p}=\epsilon^{-b}
and
g_{s}=\epsilon^{a}
, for
(a,b)>(0,0)
in the limit
\epsilon\to 0
. The exponential piece in (
182
), behaves as:

\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}~{\rm exp}\left[-r\epsilon^{a\theta_{\hat{c}(r)}-2a-6b}\right],

(185)

for
r\in\mathbb{Z}
and
n=k=l=0
. The choice of
g_{s}=\epsilon^{a}
with
a>0
is the late time, but we can keep
a\to 0
to cover the whole range in the temporal domain

-{1\over\sqrt{\Lambda}}<t\leq 0
. Even with this, the first four entries in
Table
7
suggests that the exponential piece dies-off pretty fast, implying that the contributions from the instanton sector may be made arbitrarily small. Note that the choice

\theta_{\bar{c}(r)}={2\over 3}
makes the exponential piece to be independent of
g_{s}
. This is not a classical contribution because
\theta_{\hat{c}(r)}=2
adds in the necessary quantum corrections both to the fluctuation determinants and to the exponential factors. From
(
185
), the exponential piece would still appear to die-off, but if the value of the integral coming from
{\bf I}_{1}^{(0)}(\hat{c}(r),y^{\prime\prime})
is kept small then we can have finite answers for small
r
.

{\bf I}_{1}^{(0)}(\hat{c}(r),y^{\prime\prime})
can have contributions from cubic order curvature terms, but also from the emergent Rarita-Schwinger fermionic condensates.
Although we don’t discuss the fermionic story here (see
[
9
]
for details on this), it is known for sometime now
[
2
]
that type IIB gaugino condensates on seven-branes play a crucial role in generating a positive cosmological constant solution.
The series constructed out of them is clearly convergent as one would have expected from it’s trans-series form, thus quantifying the precise contributions from the instanton sectors for this scenario. For the choice
\theta_{\bar{c}(r)}=0
, we have
\theta_{\hat{c}(r)}={8\over 3}
from (
184
). On the other hand from
Table
7
and (
185
),
we see that as long as
a>9b

-
which is late time
-
the exponential piece may be perturbatively expanded, otherwise it will die-off pretty fast. Finally the choice of
\theta_{\hat{c}(r)}={2\over 3}
doesn’t produce anything quantum so we will ignore it. Putting everything together would tell us how much do the instantons contribute to the EOM (
171
).

This brings us to the final question: where does the second EFT criterion
from (
146
) fits in the aforementioned discussion? The answer lies in the form of the two scalings
\theta_{\bar{c}(r)}
and
\theta_{\hat{c}(r)}
. We can rewrite them in the following way:

\theta_{\bar{c}(r)}=\theta^{(1)}_{\bar{c}(r)}+|\theta^{(2)}_{\bar{c}(r)}|~{\rm sign}~\theta^{(2)}_{\bar{c}(r)},~~~~~\theta_{\hat{c}(r)}=\theta^{(1)}_{\hat{c}(r)}+|\theta^{(2)}_{\hat{c}(r)}|~{\rm sign}~\theta^{(2)}_{\hat{c}(r)},

(186)

where
\theta^{(i)}_{c(r)}
for
i=1,2
are used to split the scaling
\theta_{c(r)}
into two pieces with the second one being sensitive to the second criterion of (
146
). This is captured by
{\rm sign}~\theta_{c(r)}
defined in the following way:

{\rm sign}~\theta_{c(r)}\equiv\begin{cases}~~+1~~~~~~{\rm for}~~{\partial g_{s}\over\partial t}\propto g_{s}^{+{\rm ive}}\\
~~~\\
~~-1~~~~~~{\rm for}~~{\partial g_{s}\over\partial t}\propto g_{s}^{-{\rm ive}}\end{cases}

(187)

Plugging this in either (
180
) or (
184
) would create relative minus signs implying that there are now infinite possible solutions for
\theta^{(i)}_{c(r)}
. This would clearly be a breakdown of the
g_{s}
hierarchy. The
{\rm M}_{p}
hierarchy is also broken because of the presence of the emergent localized G-fluxes in a similar vein as in

[
14
,
16
]
. Thus if the second criterion of (
146
) is not satisfied then EFT would break down because of the presence of an infinite number of operators at order
{g_{s}^{a}\over{\rm M}_{p}^{b}}
,
\forall(a,b)
with
(a,b)\in(\mathbb{Z},\mathbb{Z})
.

Therefore to summarize: the second criterion in (
146
) is absolutely essential for the consistency of the computations presented in this section done by including the instanton corrections. What about the non-local interactions? It turns out that the story is bit more subtle than what we discussed earlier. In the following we elaborate on this.

7.3.2
Revisiting the non-local interactions from (
117
) and (
169
)

In the three points mentioned just below (
117
), we cautioned the readers that (
117
)
may not
be the full answer, as there could exist the possibility of additional corrections to (
117
). However the analysis that we performed to reach to
(
117
) did not suggest any such possibilities, so the question is how and why do we need additional corrections to (
117
). Note that a similar form for the instanton terms did require additional contributions coming from the fluctuation determinants around the instantons, so therein the corrected action did have a solid reasoning for back up. On the other hand, the computation that we performed to convert (
114
) to (
117
) was aimed to get a
convergent
form for the non-local interactions without losing any information contained in (
114
). This resulted in a specific choice for the coefficients in (
117
) related to the ones from
(
114
) via the matrix
\mathbb{M}
in (
116
). The only way to relax this, yet still keeping the convergence property intact, is to convert (
117
) to a trans-series much like what we had for the instanton terms. However this begs the question as to
why
should we do this. Is there anything in particular missing from
(
117
) that requires us to change the format of (
117
)?

To see the short-comings of the non-local interactions (
117
) appearing in the action (
169
), let us go back to the terms on the eighth line of (
171
). Our aim would be to check the
g_{s}
scalings of the various terms therein using the metric scalings (
174
). For simplicity, we can restrict ourselves to the space-time directions only. The
g_{s}
scaling then becomes:

\begin{split}&\langle{\bf g}_{\rm AB}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma}\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}b_{p}\left[{\rm exp}\left({-p{\rm M}_{p}^{8}\int_{{\cal M}_{8}}d^{6}y^{\prime}~d^{2}w^{\prime}\sqrt{{\bf g}_{8}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})}\big|\mathbb{F}({\rm Y-Y^{\prime}}){\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\tilde{c}(p);\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})\big|}\right)-1\right]\\
&~=~g_{s}^{-{8\over 3}}\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}b_{p}\left[{\rm exp}\left(-p{\rm M}_{p}^{6}g_{s}^{\theta_{\tilde{c}(p)}-2}\int_{{\cal M}_{6}}d^{6}y^{\prime}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}|\mathbb{F}(y-y^{\prime}){\bf I}_{3}^{(n)}(\tilde{c}(p);y^{\prime})|g_{s}^{2n\over 3}\right)-1\right]\\
\end{split}

(188)

where
{\bf I}_{3}^{(n)}(\tilde{c}(p);y^{\prime})
is similar to
{\bf I}_{1}^{(n)}(\hat{c}(r);y^{\prime\prime})
appearing in say (
182
) but expressed using different coefficients
\tilde{c}(p)
in (
173
). Comparing the
g_{s}
scalings of this term with the ones from (
175
), we already see an insurmountable issue: the
g_{s}
scaling of the last term above can never match with the
g_{s}
scalings of (
175
)! However in the limit
g_{s}\to 0
this terms is cancelled by the exponential piece whenever
\theta_{\tilde{c}(p)}>2
, so doesn’t give enough reason to discard it yet. On the other hand, comparing the
g_{s}
scaling of the exponential piece with the ones from (
175
), we get the following value for
\theta_{\tilde{c}(p)}
:

\theta_{\tilde{c}(p)}~=~2+{{\rm log}(2r~{\rm log}~g_{s})-{\rm log}~3\over{\rm log}~g_{s}},

(189)

Figure 16:
Plot of the real and imaginary parts of the function
\theta_{\tilde{c}(p)}=2+{{\rm log}\left(-{2\over 3}~{\rm log}~g\right)\over{\rm log}~g}
in
blue
and

orange
respectively. Note that although solutions exist for
g<1
, because
{\bf Im}~\theta_{\tilde{c}(p)}=0
, they are functions of
g
. For
g>1
, clearly both

{\bf Im}~\theta_{\tilde{c}(p)}
and
{\bf Re}~\theta_{\tilde{c}(p)}
are non-zero, so no solutions exist.

with
r=-1
and
r=3
. For generic value of
g_{s}<1
this is problematic because it will make
\theta_{\tilde{c}(p)}
to be
g_{s}
dependent, which is unacceptable. In fact for
r=-1
in (
189
), solutions for
\theta_{\tilde{c}(p)}
appear to exist for
g_{s}<1
but they are still slowly varying functions of
g_{s}
as shown in
figure
16
. For
r=3
in (
189
), there are no solutions for
g_{s}<1
as shown in
figure
17
. From this figure one may see that solutions exist, albeit as functions of
g_{s}
, only for
g_{s}\geq\left[\mathbb{W}(1)\right]^{-{1\over 2}}\approx 1.328
. (
\mathbb{W}(z)
is the Lambert W-function.) This implies that
\theta_{\tilde{c}(p)}
we got in (
189
) for either values of
r
is unacceptable.

The above analysis suggests that the non-local interactions appearing in the fourth line of (
169
) cannot be the complete answer. Note that this mismatch is only visible once we used the Schwinger-Dyson equations. In fact the reason why we couldn’t match the
g_{s}
scalings was because of the absence of any operators accompanying the exponential piece. These operators would be similar to the fluctuation determinants that appear in a similar setting with the instanton terms, but now there is a difference: we can use both local and non-local operators as the fluctuation determinants now. This means we can quantify the “fluctuation determinants
"
by the following set of operators:

{\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\check{c}(p);{\bf\Xi}({\rm X}))+{\rm M}_{p}^{8}\int_{{\cal M}_{8}}d^{6}y^{\prime\prime}d^{2}w^{\prime\prime}\sqrt{{\bf g}_{8}({\rm Y}^{\prime\prime},x)}~{\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\grave{c}(p);{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime\prime},x))\mathbb{F}(y-y^{\prime\prime};w-w^{\prime\prime}),

(190)

where
{\bf Q}_{\rm pert}
is defined as in (
167
) but now with coefficients
\check{c}(p)\equiv\check{c}_{nl}(p)
and
\grave{c}(p)\equiv\grave{c}_{nl}(p)
. The other notations are:
{\rm X}=(x,y,w)
,

{\rm Y}^{\prime\prime}=(y^{\prime\prime},w^{\prime\prime})
and
{\bf\Xi}
is the set of on-shell fields as before. In the Schwinger-Dyson equations the latter has to be replaced by its expectation value
\langle{\bf\Xi}\rangle_{\sigma}
, but there is still one more subtlety remaining related to the piece
-b_{p}\sqrt{-{\bf g}_{11}({\rm X})}
that appears on the fourth line of (
169
) without an exponential factor. This is also the piece that led to an earlier mismatch with the
g_{s}
scalings. In the presence of the operators (
190
), this particular piece will lead to two set of operator series: a perturbative quantum series and a non-local quantum series. The former is what we had as
{\bf S}_{\rm pert}
in (
125
) and the latter as
{\bf S}_{\rm nloc}
in (
114
)
before
we converted them to
{\bf S}_{\rm NP}
and
{\bf S}_{\rm nloc}
in (
127
) and (
117
) respectively. The former simply renormalizes the coefficient
d_{s}
in the third line of (
169
), while the latter
-
taking the form (
117
)
-
will again have the same issue as before because of the
-1
factor. The correct way out of this is to rewrite the fourth line of (
169
) in the following way:

\begin{split}{\bf S}_{\rm nloc}({\bf\Xi})&={\rm M}_{p}^{11}\int d^{3}x~d^{6}y~d^{2}w\sqrt{-{\bf g}_{11}({\rm X})}\\
&\times\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}b_{p}\left[{\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\check{c}(p);{\bf\Xi}({\rm X}))+{\rm M}_{p}^{8}\int_{{\cal M}_{8}}d^{6}y^{\prime\prime}d^{2}w^{\prime\prime}\sqrt{{\bf g}_{8}({\rm Y}^{\prime\prime},x)}~{\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\grave{c}(p);{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime\prime},x))\mathbb{F}(y-y^{\prime\prime};w-w^{\prime\prime})\right]\\
&\times{\rm exp}\left({-p{\rm M}_{p}^{8}\int_{{\cal M}_{8}}d^{6}y^{\prime}~d^{2}w^{\prime}\sqrt{{\bf g}_{8}({\rm Y}^{\prime},x)}\big|\mathbb{F}(y-y^{\prime};w-w^{\prime}){\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\tilde{c}(p);{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime},x))\big|}\right)\\
\end{split}

(191)

where the second line above may be interpreted as the fluctuation determinants and the third line as the saddles from the non-local effects. This will effect the EOM in (
171
), and consequently the eighth, ninth and the tenth lines will get modified, including some of the entries in
Table
6
. These changes are easy to quantify and
in the following we will elaborate on them.

Let us start with the simpler piece in (
191
) where the fluctuation determinant does not depend on the non-locality factor. The Schwinger-Dyson equation corresponding to this term may be expressed in the following way:

\begin{split}&-{1\over\sqrt{{\bf g}_{11}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma})}}\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}b_{p}{\delta\over\delta\langle{\bf g}^{\rm AB}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma}}\left(\sqrt{{\bf g}_{11}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma})}{\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\check{c}(p);\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma})\right)\\
&\times~{\rm exp}\left({-p{\rm M}_{p}^{8}\int_{{\cal M}_{8}}d^{6}y^{\prime}~d^{2}w^{\prime}\sqrt{{\bf g}_{8}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})}\big|\mathbb{F}(y-y^{\prime};w-w^{\prime}){\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\tilde{c}(p);\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})\big|}\right)\\
&~=~\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}b_{p}g_{s}^{\theta_{\check{c}(p)}-{8\over 3}}\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}{\bf K}^{(r)}_{\mu\nu}(\check{c}(p);y)g_{s}^{2r\over 3}{\rm exp}\left(-p{\rm M}_{p}^{6}g_{s}^{\theta_{\tilde{c}(p)}-2}\int_{{\cal M}_{6}}d^{6}y^{\prime}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}|\mathbb{F}(y-y^{\prime}){\bf I}_{3}^{(n)}(\tilde{c}(p);y^{\prime})\rangle_{\sigma})|g_{s}^{2n\over 3}\right)\\
\end{split}

(192)

where
{\bf I}_{3}^{(n)}(\tilde{c}(p);y^{\prime})
is the same operator that we encountered in (
188
), and
{\bf K}^{(r)}_{\mu\nu}(\check{c}(p);y)
is constructed from the first line above. The dominant
g_{s}
scaling, for
r=n=0
and
p=1
, when compared to (
175
) satisfies:

\theta_{\check{c}(p)}-{2\over 3}={g_{s}^{\theta_{\tilde{c}(p)}-2}\over{\rm log}~g_{s}},

(193)

which is similar to what we had in (
178
), and motivated from there, as well as from
figures
14
and
15
, we cannot make
\theta_{\check{c}(p)}=\theta_{\tilde{c}(p)}
. One solution is that
\theta_{\check{c}(p)}={2\over 3}
, and for

{2\over 3}\leq\theta_{\tilde{c}(p)}<2
, the non-local contributions are heavily suppressed. Additionally the absence of quantum terms for the scaling
\theta_{\check{c}(p)}={2\over 3}
, along with similar arguments following (
181
), shows that (
192
)
does not
contribute significantly to the Schwinger-Dyson equations.

The next contribution would appear from the non-local fluctuation determinants from (
190
). It contains more complicated set of terms, so one might wonder if they can contribute to the Schwinger-Dyson equations. To see this let us express the EOMs in the following way:

\begin{split}&~-{\rm M}_{p}^{8}\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}\langle{\bf g}_{\rm AB}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma}\int_{{\cal M}_{8}}d^{6}y^{\prime}d^{2}w^{\prime}\sqrt{{\bf g}_{8}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})}~{\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\grave{c}(p);\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})\mathbb{F}(y-y^{\prime};w-w^{\prime})\\
&~\times~{\rm exp}\left({-p{\rm M}_{p}^{8}\int_{{\cal M}_{8}}d^{6}y^{\prime\prime}~d^{2}w^{\prime\prime}\sqrt{{\bf g}_{8}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})}\big|\mathbb{F}(y-y^{\prime\prime};w-w^{\prime\prime}){\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\tilde{c}(p);\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})\big|}\right)\\
&~=~{\rm M}_{p}^{6}\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}b_{p}g_{s}^{\theta_{\grave{c}(p)}-{14\over 3}}\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\int_{{\cal M}_{6}}d^{6}y^{\prime}{\bf L}^{(r)}_{\mu\nu}(\grave{c}(p);y)\mathbb{F}(y-y^{\prime})g_{s}^{2r\over 3}~{\rm exp}\left(-p{\rm M}_{p}^{6}g_{s}^{\theta_{\tilde{c}(p)}-2}\int_{{\cal M}_{6}}d^{6}y^{\prime}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}|\mathbb{F}(y-y^{\prime}){\bf I}_{3}^{(n)}(\tilde{c}(p);y^{\prime})|g_{s}^{2n\over 3}\right)\\
\end{split}

(194)

where
{\bf I}_{3}^{(n)}(\tilde{c}(p);y^{\prime})
is the same operator that we encountered earlier, but
{\bf L}^{(r)}_{\mu\nu}(\grave{c}(p);y)
is new and is constructed from the first line above. A naive
g_{s}
scaling of the dominant term for
r=n=0
and
p=1
with the
g_{s}
scalings from (
175
) gives us:

\theta_{\grave{c}(p)}-{8\over 3}={g_{s}^{\theta_{\tilde{c}(p)}-2}\over{\rm log}~g_{s}},

(195)

which while doesn’t have the same problem that we faced with (
188
), doesn’t also provide a
g_{s}
independent solution
for
\theta_{\grave{c}(p)}=\theta_{\tilde{c}(p)}
. This is of course expected, and therefore we can keep
\theta_{\grave{c}(p)}\neq\theta_{\tilde{c}(p)}
and assume the following solution for
\theta_{\grave{c}(p)}
:

\theta_{\grave{c}(p)}={8\over 3},

(196)

implying that for
{2\over 3}\leq\theta_{\tilde{c}(p)}<2
the exponential piece will continue to be suppressed for
p\geq 1
at late time. For
\theta_{\tilde{c}(p)}\geq 2
, one may perturbatively expand the exponential piece in powers of
g_{s}
. They will then contribute to the higher order Schwinger-Dyson equations. Again, we see that the contributing terms come typically from quartic order curvature terms satisfying (
196
). In fact we expect similar contributions from another related term from the non-local action (
191
), namely:

\begin{split}&-{\rm M}_{p}^{8}\int d^{6}y^{\prime}d^{2}w^{\prime}\sqrt{\bf g_{11}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma}\over{\bf g}_{11}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma}}\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}{\delta\over\delta\langle{\bf g}_{\rm AB}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma}}\left(\sqrt{{\bf g}_{8}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma})}~{\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\grave{c}(p);\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma})\right)\mathbb{F}(y^{\prime}-y;w^{\prime}-w)\\
&~\times~{\rm exp}\left({-p{\rm M}_{p}^{8}\int_{{\cal M}_{8}}d^{6}y^{\prime\prime}~d^{2}w^{\prime\prime}\sqrt{{\bf g}_{8}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})}\big|\mathbb{F}(y^{\prime}-y^{\prime\prime};w^{\prime}-w^{\prime\prime}){\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\tilde{c}(p);\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})\big|}\right)\\
&~=~{\rm M}_{p}^{6}\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}b_{p}g_{s}^{\theta_{\grave{c}(p)}-{14\over 3}}\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\int_{{\cal M}_{6}}d^{6}y^{\prime}{\bf M}^{(r)}_{\mu\nu}(\grave{c}(p);y)\mathbb{F}(y-y^{\prime})g_{s}^{2r\over 3}~{\rm exp}\left(-p{\rm M}_{p}^{6}g_{s}^{\theta_{\tilde{c}(p)}-2}\int_{{\cal M}_{6}}d^{6}y^{\prime\prime}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}|\mathbb{F}(y^{\prime}-y^{\prime\prime}){\bf I}_{3}^{(n)}(\tilde{c}(p);y^{\prime\prime})|g_{s}^{2n\over 3}\right)\\
\end{split}

(197)

which has similar behavior as (
194
) except with a different function
{\bf M}^{(r)}_{\mu\nu}(\grave{c}(p);y)
and a different distribution of integrals in the exponential term. This also means that (
196
) continues to be the quantum scaling here too, and the term could contribute non-trivially as long as
\theta_{\tilde{c}(p)}\geq 2
. The dominant contribution would come from say a quartic order curvature piece from the fluctuation determinants and a
g_{s}
independent
\theta_{\tilde{c}(p)}=2
piece from the exponential factor.

There is still one more contribution to the Schwinger-Dyson equation coming from a mixture of the terms from the fluctuation determinants and the exponential factors. This may be presented in the following way:

\begin{split}&{\rm M}_{p}^{8}\int d^{6}y^{\prime}~d^{2}w^{\prime}\sqrt{{\bf g}_{11}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})\over{\bf g}_{11}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma})}~\mathbb{F}({\rm Y^{\prime}-Y})\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}pb_{p}~{\delta\over\delta\langle{\bf g}^{\rm AB}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma}}\left[\sqrt{{\bf g}_{8}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma})}{\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\tilde{c}(p);\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma})\right]\\
&~\times\left[{\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\check{c}(p);\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})+{\rm M}_{p}^{8}\int_{{\cal M}_{8}}d^{6}y^{\prime\prime}d^{2}w^{\prime\prime}\sqrt{{\bf g}_{8}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})}~{\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\grave{c}(p);\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})\mathbb{F}(y^{\prime}-y^{\prime\prime};w^{\prime}-w^{\prime\prime})\right]\\
&~\times~{\rm exp}\left({-p{\rm M}_{p}^{8}\int_{{\cal M}_{8}}d^{6}y^{\prime\prime\prime}~d^{2}w^{\prime\prime\prime}\sqrt{{\bf g}_{8}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime\prime\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})}\big|\mathbb{F}(y^{\prime}-y^{\prime\prime\prime};w^{\prime}-w^{\prime\prime\prime}){\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\tilde{c}(p);\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime\prime\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})\big|}\right)\\
&={\rm M}_{p}^{6}\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}pb_{p}\int_{{\cal M}_{6}}d^{6}y^{\prime}g_{s}^{\theta_{\tilde{c}(p)}-{14\over 3}}~{\rm exp}\left(-p{\rm M}_{p}^{6}g_{s}^{\theta_{\tilde{c}(p)}-2}\int_{{\cal M}_{6}}d^{6}y^{\prime\prime\prime}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}|\mathbb{F}(y^{\prime}-y^{\prime\prime\prime}){\bf I}_{3}^{(n)}(\tilde{c}(p);y^{\prime\prime\prime})|g_{s}^{2n\over 3}\right)\\
&~\times\left[\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}{\bf P}_{\mu\nu}^{(k)}(\tilde{c}(p),\check{c}(p);y^{\prime})~g_{s}^{\theta_{\check{c}(p)}+{2k\over 3}}+{\rm M}_{p}^{6}\int_{{\cal M}_{6}}d^{6}y^{\prime\prime}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}{\bf Q}_{\mu\nu}^{(k)}(\tilde{c}(p),\grave{c}(p);y^{\prime\prime})~\mathbb{F}(y^{\prime}-y^{\prime\prime})~g_{s}^{\theta_{\grave{c}(p)}+{2k\over 3}}\right]\mathbb{F}(y^{\prime}-y)\\
\end{split}

(198)

where
{\bf P}_{\mu\nu}^{(k)}(\tilde{c}(p),\check{c}(p);y^{\prime})
and

{\bf Q}_{\mu\nu}^{(k)}(\tilde{c}(p),\grave{c}(p);y^{\prime\prime})
are two new functions appearing from the local and non-local pieces from the fluctuation determinants; and
{\bf I}_{3}^{(n)}(\tilde{c}(p);y^{\prime\prime\prime})
is the same function that appeared earlier. One may also easily see that the quantum scalings now satisfy the following set of relations:

\theta_{\tilde{c}(p)}+\theta_{\check{c}(p)}={8\over 3},~~~~\theta_{\tilde{c}(p)}+\theta_{\grave{c}(p)}={8\over 3},

(199)

where each piece is similar to what we encountered in (
184
), and therefore would be classified individually as in
Table
7
. To summarize: including the fluctuation determinants to modify the non-local interactions as in (
191
), all ambiguities seem to go away and consistent matching of the
g_{s}
scalings may be easily performed.

7.3.3
Five-brane instantons wrapped on six-manifold
{\cal M}_{4}\times{\mathbb{T}^{2}\over{\cal G}}

A detailed study done in the previous section instructed us how to deal with five-brane instantons wrapped on the six-dimensional base
{\cal M}_{6}
of the eight-manifold
{\cal M}_{8}={\cal M}_{6}\times{\mathbb{T}^{2}\over{\cal G}}
. We saw the contributions from both the non-perturbative as well as the non-local terms in the action (
169
), although a more careful study revealed that (
169
) needs to be modified because the non-local interactions given in (
117
) are incomplete. The action that actually takes care of all the aforementioned subtleties and from where the correct Schwinger-Dyson equations can be derived is given by the following:

\begin{split}&\hat{\bf S}_{\rm tot}({\bf\Xi})={\bf S}_{\rm kin}({\bf\Xi})+{\bf S}_{\rm NP}({\bf\Xi})+{\bf S}_{\rm nloc}({\bf\Xi})\\
&={\rm M}_{p}^{9}\int d^{3}x~d^{6}y~d^{2}w\sqrt{-{\bf g}_{11}({\rm X})}\left[{\bf R}_{11}({\rm X})+{\bf G}_{4}({\rm X})\wedge\ast_{11}{\bf G}_{4}({\rm X})\right]+{\rm M}_{p}^{9}\int{\bf C}_{3}({\rm X})\wedge{\bf G}_{4}({\rm X})\wedge{\bf G}_{4}({\rm X)}+{\rm M}_{p}^{3}\int{\bf C}_{3}({\rm X})\wedge\mathbb{X}_{8}({\rm X})\\
&+{\rm M}_{p}^{11}\int d^{3}x~d^{6}y~d^{2}w\sqrt{-{\bf g}_{11}({\rm X})}~\sum_{s=0}^{\infty}d_{s}~{\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\bar{c}(s);{\bf\Xi}({\rm X}))~{\rm exp}\left(-s{\rm M}_{p}^{8}\int_{0}^{y}\int_{0}^{w}d^{6}y^{\prime}~d^{2}w^{\prime}\sqrt{{\bf g}_{8}({\rm Y}^{\prime},x)}\big|{\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\hat{c}(s);{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime},x))\big|\right)\\
&+{\rm M}_{p}^{11}\int d^{3}x~d^{6}y~d^{2}w\sqrt{-{\bf g}_{11}({\rm X})}\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}b_{p}~{\rm exp}\left({-p{\rm M}_{p}^{8}\int_{{\cal M}_{8}}d^{6}y^{\prime}~d^{2}w^{\prime}\sqrt{{\bf g}_{8}({\rm Y}^{\prime},x)}\big|\mathbb{F}(y-y^{\prime};w-w^{\prime}){\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\tilde{c}(p);{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime},x))\big|}\right)\\
&\times\left[{\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\check{c}(p);{\bf\Xi}({\rm X}))+{\rm M}_{p}^{8}\int_{{\cal M}_{8}}d^{6}y^{\prime\prime}d^{2}w^{\prime\prime}\sqrt{{\bf g}_{8}({\rm Y}^{\prime\prime},x)}~{\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\grave{c}(p);{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime\prime},x))\mathbb{F}(y-y^{\prime\prime};w-w^{\prime\prime})\right],\\
\end{split}

(200)

where
{\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(c;{\bf\Xi}(x,y,w))
is defined in terms of the on-shell degrees of freedom
{\bf\Xi}(x,y,w)
in (
167
) with
c\equiv\{c_{nl}\}
forming the set of coefficients for the perturbative series in (
167
). These set of coefficients may be used to differentiate between the various contributions from the instanton and the non-local saddles and their corresponding fluctuation determinants as shown in (
200
). For the five-brane instantons wrapped on the six-manifold base, the perturbative series may be expressed as (
173
). On the other hand, to study the five-brane instantons wrapped on the toroidal manifold and a four-cycle in the six-dimensional base one can take
{\cal M}_{6}
locally as
{\cal M}_{4}\times{\cal M}_{2}
, and study the instantons wrapped on
{\cal M}_{4}\times{\mathbb{T}^{2}\over{\cal G}}
. These instantons are related to the three-branes instantons in the type IIB side, which we can call as the KKLT instantons
[
2
]
. For such a case the perturbative series may be defined as:

{\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(c;{\bf\Xi}(y^{m},y^{\alpha},w,x))\equiv{\bf Q}_{\rm pert}^{\rm KKLT}(c;{\bf\Xi}(y^{m},w,x))~{\delta^{2}(y^{\alpha}-y^{\alpha}_{o})\over{\rm M}_{p}^{2}\sqrt{\bar{\bf g}_{2}(y^{\alpha},x)}},

(201)

where
y^{m}\in{\cal M}_{4},y^{\alpha}\in{\cal M}_{2},w\in{\mathbb{T}^{2}\over{\cal G}}
and
x\in{\bf R}^{2,1}
. We have also assume that
{\bf g}_{8}(y^{m},y^{\alpha},w,x)={\bf g}_{4}(y^{m},x)\bar{\bf g}_{2}(y^{\alpha},x){\bf g}_{2}(w,x)
, where the coordinate dependence specifies the corresponding sub-manifold for which we take the metric determinant.

The analysis with the KKLT instantons is very similar to the ones we did with the BBS instantons, except that the determinant of the metric for
{\cal M}_{4}\times{\mathbb{T}^{2}\over{\cal G}}
has zero scaling with
g_{s}
, so the quantum terms do not contribute much to the EOMs. Therefore
we will not go in details here and leave the analysis for the diligent readers. The procedure follows the strategy we laid out in section
7.3.2
, namely substitute (
201
) in the Schwinger-Dyson equations and then compute the contributions from all the terms
64
64

64

There would also be contributions from the two-brane instantons, as discussed briefly in
[
15
]
, including other instantons that may not have simple brane interpretations. For the latter one could, for example, also expect contributions from the stable non-BPS states in the theory that we haven’t touched here. Additionally all the aforementioned instantons are
real
, but the resurgence story also includes
complex
instantons coming from the complex turning points in a potential with Minkowski minima (with possible scale-separated AdS minima as the scale-separated ones do not appear to allow for an EFT description). All of these should come together to explain the various instantonic saddles appearing in the action (
200
). A detailed analysis of these effects is clearly beyond the scope of this paper and will be discussed elsewhere.
. The Schwinger-Dyson equation coming from the action (
200
) may be succinctly presented as:

\begin{split}&{\bf R}_{\rm AB}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma})-{1\over 2}\langle{\bf g}_{\rm AB}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma}{\bf R}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma})\\
&=~{2\over\sqrt{{\bf g}_{11}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma})}}{\delta\over\delta\langle{\bf g}^{\rm AB}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma}}\left(\sqrt{{\bf g}_{11}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma})}{\bf G}_{4}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma})\wedge\ast_{11}{\bf G}_{4}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma})\right)\\
&-~{2{\rm M}_{p}^{2}\over\sqrt{{\bf g}_{11}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma})}}\sum_{s=0}^{\infty}d_{s}{\delta\over\delta\langle{\bf g}^{\rm AB}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma}}\left[\sqrt{{\bf g}_{11}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma})}{\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\bar{c}(s);\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma})\right]\\
&\times~{\rm exp}\left(-s{\rm M}_{p}^{8}\int_{0}^{y}\int_{0}^{w}d^{6}y^{\prime}~d^{2}w^{\prime}\sqrt{{\bf g}_{8}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})}\big|{\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\hat{c}(s);\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})\big|\right)\\
&+~{\rm M}_{p}^{10}\int d^{6}y^{\prime}~d^{2}w^{\prime}\sqrt{{\bf g}_{11}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})\over{\bf g}_{11}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma})}~\sum_{s=0}^{\infty}~sd_{s}~{\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\bar{c}(s);\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y^{\prime}},x)\rangle_{\sigma})\\
&\times~{\delta\over\delta\langle{\bf g}^{\rm AB}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma}}\left[\sqrt{{\bf g}_{8}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma})}{\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\hat{c}(s);\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma})\right]~\Theta(y^{\prime}-y)~\Theta(w^{\prime}-z)\\
&\times~{\rm exp}\left(-s{\rm M}_{p}^{8}\int_{0}^{y^{\prime}}\int_{0}^{w^{\prime}}d^{6}y^{\prime\prime}~d^{2}w^{\prime\prime}\sqrt{{\bf g}_{8}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})}\big|{\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\hat{c}(s);\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})\big|\right)\\
&-{1\over\sqrt{{\bf g}_{11}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma})}}\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}b_{p}{\delta\over\delta\langle{\bf g}^{\rm AB}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma}}\left(\sqrt{{\bf g}_{11}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma})}{\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\check{c}(p);\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma})\right)\\
&\times~{\rm exp}\left({-p{\rm M}_{p}^{8}\int_{{\cal M}_{8}}d^{6}y^{\prime}~d^{2}w^{\prime}\sqrt{{\bf g}_{8}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})}\big|\mathbb{F}(y-y^{\prime};w-w^{\prime}){\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\tilde{c}(p);\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})\big|}\right)\\
&~-{\rm M}_{p}^{8}\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}\langle{\bf g}_{\rm AB}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma}\int_{{\cal M}_{8}}d^{6}y^{\prime}d^{2}w^{\prime}\sqrt{{\bf g}_{8}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})}~{\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\grave{c}(p);\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})\mathbb{F}(y-y^{\prime};w-w^{\prime})\\
&~\times~{\rm exp}\left({-p{\rm M}_{p}^{8}\int_{{\cal M}_{8}}d^{6}y^{\prime\prime}~d^{2}w^{\prime\prime}\sqrt{{\bf g}_{8}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})}\big|\mathbb{F}(y-y^{\prime\prime};w-w^{\prime\prime}){\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\tilde{c}(p);\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})\big|}\right)\\
&-{\rm M}_{p}^{8}\int d^{6}y^{\prime}d^{2}w^{\prime}\sqrt{\bf g_{11}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma}\over{\bf g}_{11}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma}}\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}{\delta\over\delta\langle{\bf g}_{\rm AB}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma}}\left(\sqrt{{\bf g}_{8}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma})}~{\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\grave{c}(p);\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma})\right)\mathbb{F}(y^{\prime}-y;w^{\prime}-w)\\
&~\times~{\rm exp}\left({-p{\rm M}_{p}^{8}\int_{{\cal M}_{8}}d^{6}y^{\prime\prime}~d^{2}w^{\prime\prime}\sqrt{{\bf g}_{8}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})}\big|\mathbb{F}(y^{\prime}-y^{\prime\prime};w^{\prime}-w^{\prime\prime}){\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\tilde{c}(p);\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})\big|}\right)\\
&~+~{\rm M}_{p}^{8}\int d^{6}y^{\prime}~d^{2}w^{\prime}\sqrt{{\bf g}_{11}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})\over{\bf g}_{11}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma})}~\mathbb{F}({\rm Y^{\prime}-Y})\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}pb_{p}~{\delta\over\delta\langle{\bf g}^{\rm AB}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma}}\left[\sqrt{{\bf g}_{8}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma})}{\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\tilde{c}(p);\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma})\right]\\
&~\times\left[{\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\check{c}(p);\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})+{\rm M}_{p}^{8}\int_{{\cal M}_{8}}d^{6}y^{\prime\prime}d^{2}w^{\prime\prime}\sqrt{{\bf g}_{8}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})}~{\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\grave{c}(p);\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})\mathbb{F}(y^{\prime}-y^{\prime\prime};w^{\prime}-w^{\prime\prime})\right]\\
&~\times~{\rm exp}\left({-p{\rm M}_{p}^{8}\int_{{\cal M}_{8}}d^{6}y^{\prime\prime\prime}~d^{2}w^{\prime\prime\prime}\sqrt{{\bf g}_{8}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime\prime\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})}\big|\mathbb{F}(y^{\prime}-y^{\prime\prime\prime};w^{\prime}-w^{\prime\prime\prime}){\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\tilde{c}(p);\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime\prime\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})\big|}\right)\\
\end{split}

(202)

which modifies (
171
) appropriately. Therefore instead of taking the configuration (
174
), we can take the configuration from (
85
) with the simplifying conditions from (
92
), except now
\alpha(t)\neq 0
and
\beta(t)\neq 0
. Allowing a non-zero
\alpha(t)
and
\beta(t)
could in principle lead to late time singularities, which we should avoid. Since we have chosen
f_{1}(t)
as the defining function for all the flux and the metric components, it will be useful to also define
\alpha(t)
and
\beta(t)
in terms of
f_{1}(t)
in the following way:

\alpha(t)=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}l_{n}\left(f_{1}(t)-{3a\over 2}\right)^{n},~~~\beta(t)=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}p_{n}\left(f_{1}(t)-{3a\over 2}\right)^{n},

(203)

with appropriate choice for the coefficients
(l_{n},p_{n})
such that

(\alpha(t),\beta(t))<\left({2\over 3},{2\over 3}\right)
. The latter requirement is to ensure the correct limit of type IIB theory from M-theory. Note that as
t\to 0
, both
\alpha(t)
and
\beta(t)
vanishes as can be inferred from section
5.3
. The Einstein term in (
202
) scales in the following way:

{\bf R}_{\rm AB}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma})-{1\over 2}\langle{\bf g}_{\rm AB}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma}{\bf R}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma})=~{1\over g_{s}^{2}}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}{\bf G}^{(k)}_{\mu\nu}(y,w)~\check{g}_{s}^{{2k\over 3}{\rm L}_{1}(f_{1}(t))+{\rm log~corrections}},

(204)

which is significantly different from the scaling in (
175
) because of the presence of
\hat{g}_{s}\equiv g_{s}^{f_{1}(t)}
,
{\rm L}_{1}(f_{1}(t))
and log corrections. They provide sub-dominant contributions in
(
204
) because at late time
f_{1}(t)
approaches a constant value of
{3a\over 2}
, and log corrections are typically very small. The explicit form for
{\rm L}_{1}(f_{1}(t))

can
be derived but the analysis is quite technical and the readers may get the derivation from
[
9
]
. One may now try to compare the
g_{s}
scalings of the Einstein term with all the other terms from (
202
). We will however not do the detailed analysis here, but only discuss one such term and leave the others for future work. The term that we will like to study is the following:

\begin{split}&{\rm M}_{p}^{8}\int d^{6}y^{\prime}~d^{2}w^{\prime}\sqrt{{\bf g}_{11}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})\over{\bf g}_{11}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma})}~\mathbb{F}({\rm Y^{\prime}-Y})\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}pb_{p}~{\delta\over\delta\langle{\bf g}^{\rm AB}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma}}\left[\sqrt{{\bf g}_{8}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma})}{\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\tilde{c}(p);\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm X})\rangle_{\sigma})\right]\\
&~\times\left[{\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\check{c}(p);\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})+{\rm M}_{p}^{8}\int_{{\cal M}_{8}}d^{6}y^{\prime\prime}d^{2}w^{\prime\prime}\sqrt{{\bf g}_{8}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})}~{\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\grave{c}(p);\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})\mathbb{F}(y^{\prime}-y^{\prime\prime};w^{\prime}-w^{\prime\prime})\right]\\
&~\times~{\rm exp}\left({-p{\rm M}_{p}^{8}\int_{{\cal M}_{8}}d^{6}y^{\prime\prime\prime}~d^{2}w^{\prime\prime\prime}\sqrt{{\bf g}_{8}(\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime\prime\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})}\big|\mathbb{F}(y^{\prime}-y^{\prime\prime\prime};w^{\prime}-w^{\prime\prime\prime}){\bf Q}_{\rm pert}(\tilde{c}(p);\langle{\bf\Xi}({\rm Y}^{\prime\prime\prime},x)\rangle_{\sigma})\big|}\right)\\
&={\rm M}_{p}^{6}\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}pb_{p}\int_{\widetilde{\cal M}_{6}}d^{4}y^{\prime}d^{2}w^{\prime}g_{s}^{\theta_{\tilde{c}(p)}(g_{s})-{14\over 3}}\check{g}_{s}^{{\rm log~corrections}}\\
&\times{\rm exp}\left(-p{\rm M}_{p}^{6}g_{s}^{\theta_{\tilde{c}(p)}(g_{s})-2}\check{g}_{s}^{{\rm log~corrections}}\int_{\widetilde{\cal M}_{6}}d^{4}y^{\prime\prime\prime}d^{2}w^{\prime\prime\prime}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}|\mathbb{F}({\rm Y}^{\prime}-{\rm Y}^{\prime\prime\prime}){\bf I}_{3}^{(n)}(\tilde{c}(p);{\rm Y}^{\prime\prime\prime})|\check{g}_{s}^{{2n\over 3}{\rm L}_{2}(f_{1}(t))}\right)\\
&\times\Bigg[\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}{\bf P}_{\mu\nu}^{(k)}(\tilde{c}(p),\check{c}(p);y^{\prime})~g_{s}^{\theta_{\check{c}(p)}(g_{s})}\check{g}_{s}^{{2k\over 3}{\rm L}_{3}(f_{1}(t))+{\rm log~corrections}}\\
&+{\rm M}_{p}^{6}\int_{\widetilde{\cal M}_{6}}d^{4}y^{\prime\prime}d^{2}w^{\prime\prime}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}{\bf Q}_{\mu\nu}^{(k)}(\tilde{c}(p),\grave{c}(p);{\rm Y}^{\prime\prime})~\mathbb{F}({\rm Y}^{\prime}-{\rm Y}^{\prime\prime})~g_{s}^{\theta_{\grave{c}(p)}(g_{s})}\check{g}_{s}^{{2k\over 3}{\rm L}_{4}(f_{1}(t))+{\rm log~corrections}}\Bigg]\mathbb{F}({\rm Y}^{\prime}-{\rm Y})\\
\end{split}

(205)

where
{\rm Y}=(y,w)
and
\widetilde{\cal M}_{6}={\cal M}_{4}\times{\mathbb{T}^{2}\over{\cal G}}
. The matching of the
g_{s}
scalings with the ones from (
204
) is now more complicated because the quantum scaling
\theta_{c}
of (
167
) is now a function of
g_{s}
itself because of its temporal dependence. In addition to that we now have three new functions of
f_{1}(t)
, namely
{\rm L}_{i}(f_{1}(t))
for
i=2,3,4
, plus the expected log corrections. The dominant scaling appears from
n=k=0
and, with the log corrections being very small, we have a similar relation like (
199
):

\theta_{\tilde{c}(p)}(g_{s})+\theta_{\check{c}(p)}(g_{s})={8\over 3},~~~~\theta_{\tilde{c}(p)}(g_{s})+\theta_{\grave{c}(p)}(g_{s})={8\over 3},

(206)

except that the LHS are functions of
g_{s}
. As it stands, (
206
) doesn’t make any sense because the RHS are
g_{s}
independent factors of
{8\over 3}
whereas the LHS are functions of
g_{s}
, and therefore functions of the conformal time
t
. On the other hand, if
\theta_{{c}}(g_{s})
are
very slowly
varying functions of time
then (
206
) could still make sense and using it we can construct the required quantum terms that would support a configuration like (
85
) with the simplifying conditions (
92
). Recall that, one of the requirement on the temporally varying cosmological constant in section
5.3
is to take a very slow variation. We now see that this is a self-consistent assumption from various angles. What this means with respect to the DESI BAO result
[
10
]
remains to be seen, although issues pointed out in
[
21
]
may no longer persist. We will discuss more on this in future publication.

\theta_{\tilde{c}(p)}={2}+{{\rm log}\left({2}~{\rm log}~g\right)\over{\rm log}~g}
